Remark 8.16
.

There is the third way for proving Theorem
8.15
.

(iii)

Method 3: reconstruction theorem.

As we explained above, both in Method 1 and in Method 2,
Theorem
8.15
is proved
by case-by-case direct computation.
On the other hand, in Method 3, we can show the theorem
in a uniformed way.

In this method, starting with a given diagram
\Gamma=\Gamma(R,G)
in Tables

4

\sim

8
preceding Theorem
8.12
,
we reconstruct the mERS
(R,G)
as follows.

1.

Consider the real vector space
\widehat{F}
spanned by all the nodes in

\Gamma
equipped with the symmetric bilinear form
\widehat{I}
on
\widehat{F}

defined from the information of the edges in
\Gamma
.
Note that the dimension of
\widehat{F}
is nothing but the cardinality of the set

|\Gamma|
of all nodes in
\Gamma
.
Let
\hat{r}_{\alpha}\in O(\widehat{F},\widehat{I})
be the reflection attached
to
\alpha\in|\Gamma|
,
\widehat{W}
be the group generated by

\hat{r}_{\alpha}\ (\alpha\in|\Gamma|)
and
\widehat{R}:=\widehat{W}.|\Gamma|
.

2.

Let
\hat{c}
be a product of all reflections

\hat{r}_{\alpha}\ (\alpha\in|\Gamma|)
in a suitable order
7
7

7

An element

\hat{c}
is called a
pre-Coxeter element
.
. Take the Jordan decomposition

\hat{c}=SU
of
\hat{c}
,
where
S
is the semisimple part of
\hat{c}
and
U
is the unipotent part,
respectively. Set

F_{\Gamma}=\widehat{F}/\mathrm{Im}(U-\mathrm{id})
and let
I_{\Gamma}

be a bilinear form on
F_{\Gamma}
induced from
\widehat{I}
.
Let
\widehat{G}
be the subspace of
\widehat{F}
spanned by vectors

\alpha_{i}^{\ast}-\alpha_{i}\ (\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{w}}|)
and

(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}-2\alpha_{j}\ (\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|)
. Then,
the image
G_{\Gamma}:=\pi_{\mathrm{Im}(U-\mathrm{id})}(\widehat{G})
is
a one dimensional subspace of
\mathrm{rad}(I_{\Gamma})
.

3.

It can be shown that the image

R_{\Gamma}
of
\widehat{R}
under the canonical projection

\pi_{\mathrm{Im}(U-\mathrm{id})}:\widehat{F}\to F_{\Gamma}

is an elliptic root system belonging to
(F_{\Gamma},G_{\Gamma})
.
Furthermore, the pair
(R_{\Gamma},G_{\Gamma})
is
a mERS whose elliptic diagram is isomorphic to
(R,G)
.

Thus, it is shown that, for every diagram
\Gamma
in Tables
4

\sim

8
preceding Theorem
8.12
, a mERS whose
elliptic diagram is
\Gamma
, exists, and
Theorem
8.15
is proved.

Note that the above method (Method 3) is a non-reduced analogue of the result of
K. Saito for
(R,G)
with the reduced affine quotient
R/G
(
[
Sai85
]
,
\lx@sectionsign
9),
and the detail for our case will be explained in our forthcoming paper.

9.
Structure of reduced pairs

Let
(R,G)
be a mERS with the non-reduced affine quotient
R/G
,
and
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})
a reduced pair of
R
. The types of the reduced mERSs

(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
and
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
are already given in

\lx@sectionsign

6.3
.
On the other hand, the isomorphism classes of mERSs are classified in
Theorems
2.9
and
8.15
by their elliptic diagrams.
Therefore, we arrive at the following problem.

Problem 2
.

Can we extract the types of
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
and
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
from the elliptic
diagram
\Gamma(R,G)
?

The aim of this subsection is to solve this problem.
After detailed study on the structures of
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
and
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
, we will give
an answer for this problem in
\lx@sectionsign

9.4

(see Proposition
9.11
).

9.1.
Reduced pair and Elliptic diagram

Let us rewrite the results of Proposition
7.20
in terms of the elliptic
diagrams.
Recall the description (
92
) of
R
and (
93
):

R=\Big(\bigsqcup_{\gamma\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}].\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}(\gamma+\mathbb{Z}k(\gamma)a)\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigsqcup_{\gamma\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}].(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}\setminus\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}})^{\ast}}(\gamma+\mathbb{Z}k(\gamma)a)\Big),

(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}\setminus\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}})^{\ast}=\{(\alpha^{\prime})^{\ast}\,|\,\alpha\in\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},\,2\overline{\alpha}\in R/G\},

respectively. Hence, we have

(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}\setminus\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}})^{\ast}=\{(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}\,|\,\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|\}.

Note that, if
\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|
is a collapsed node,
the node
(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}
does not appear in
\Gamma(R,G)
.

For
\alpha_{i}\in\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}=|\Gamma^{\downarrow}|
, set

\displaystyle R(\alpha_{i})=

\displaystyle\bigcup_{w\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}]}(w(\alpha_{i})+\mathbb{Z}k(\alpha_{i})a),

\displaystyle R((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast})=

\displaystyle\bigcup_{w\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}]}(w((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast})+\mathbb{Z}k((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast})a).

Thus, we can rewrite (
92
) as

(123)

R=\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|}R(\alpha_{i})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|}R(\alpha_{j})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|}R((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})\Big).

Set

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{g}^{g}

\displaystyle=\{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|\,|\,\text{$\alpha_{j}$ is a gray node, $(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}$ is a gray node}\},

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{g}^{b}

\displaystyle=\{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|\,|\,\text{$\alpha_{j}$ is a gray node, $(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}$ is a black node}\},

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{b}^{g}

\displaystyle=\{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|\,|\,\text{$\alpha_{j}$ is a black node, $(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}$ is a gray node}\},

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{b}^{b}

\displaystyle=\{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|\,|\,\text{$\alpha_{j}$ is a black node, $(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}$ is a black node}\}

and

\displaystyle|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|

\displaystyle=|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|\setminus|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|.

By the observation (b) in
\lx@sectionsign

8.4
, we know that

\alpha_{j}\in|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m})^{\mathrm{nw}}|
is a black node.
By using these notations, (
91
) can be rewritten as

(124)

\begin{split}&R((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})\\
=&\begin{cases}\bigcup_{w\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}]}(2w(\alpha_{j})+(2\mathbb{Z}+1)k(\alpha_{j})a)&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{g}^{g}$},\\
\bigcup_{w\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}]}(2w(\alpha_{j})+(4\mathbb{Z}+2)k(\alpha_{j})a)&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{g}^{b}$},\\
\bigcup_{w\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}]}(2w(\alpha_{j})+\mathbb{Z}k(\alpha_{j})a)&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{b}^{g}$},\\
\bigcup_{w\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}]}(2w(\alpha_{j})+2\mathbb{Z}k(\alpha_{j})a)&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{b}^{b}$ or
$|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|$}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
(R,G)
be a mERS with the non-reduced quotient
R/G
and
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})
be
the reduced pair of
R
. We have

(125)

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}=\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{w}|}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})\Big),

(126)

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}=\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{w}|}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})\Big),

where, for
\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|
and
\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|
,

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i}):=R(\alpha_{i}),\quad R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j}):=R(\alpha_{j}),

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}):=\begin{cases}R((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{g}$},\\
\emptyset&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{b}$},\\
\bigcup_{w\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}]}(2w(\alpha_{j})+(2\mathbb{Z}+1)k(\alpha_{j})a)&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{g}$},\\
\emptyset&\begin{array}[]{l}\!\!\!\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{b}$}\\
\;\text{or $|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|$},\end{array}\end{cases}

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i}):=R(\alpha_{i}),

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j}):=\begin{cases}R(\alpha_{j}),&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{g}$},\\
\bigcup_{w\in W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}]}(w(\alpha_{j})+2\mathbb{Z}k(\alpha_{j})a)&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{b}$},\\
\emptyset&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{g}$},\\
\emptyset&\begin{array}[]{l}\!\!\!\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{b}$}\\
\;\text{or $|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|$},\end{array}\end{cases}

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}):=R((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}).

Proof.

Recalling the definitions of
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
and
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
, it is immediate to see the following
equalities:

\displaystyle R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})=

\displaystyle R(\alpha_{i})\cap R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},\qquad R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j})=R(\alpha_{j})\cap R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},

\displaystyle R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})=

\displaystyle R((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})\cap R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},

\displaystyle R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})=

\displaystyle R(\alpha_{i})\cap R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},\qquad R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j})=R(\alpha_{j})\cap R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},

\displaystyle R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})=

\displaystyle R((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})\cap R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}.

Hence, the proposition is obtained.
∎

Corollary 9.2
.

For a mERS
(R,G)
, the following are equivalent
:

(a)

R
is reduced.

(b)

There is no black node in its elliptic diagram
\Gamma(R,G)
.

.

Proof.

If
\Gamma(R,G)
has a black node, it is obvious that
R
is not reduced.
Thus, we have (a)
\Longrightarrow
(b). Conversely, assume
\Gamma(R,G)

has no black node. By the proposition above, we have

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})=R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})\quad\text{for every }\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|,

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j})=R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j})\quad\text{and}\quad R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})=R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})\quad\text{for every }\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|.

Thus, it is obtained that
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}=R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
. This means
R
is reduced.
∎

By this corollary and the list of the isomorphism classes on mERSs
in the previous subsection, the list of reduced mERS is obtained:

No.1 and No. 6 in Table
4
,  No.1 and No. 6 in Table
5
,

No.1 in Table
6
,  No.3 in Table
7
, No.1 in Table
8
.

Note that we have not proved the existence of such root systems yet (c.f.
Problem
1
in the next subsection).

In the rest of this subsection, assume
(R,G)
is non-reduced.
Note that this assumption is equivalent to that
there is a black node in
\Gamma(R,G)
by Corollary
9.2
.
In the following, we study the reduced mERSs

(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
and
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
individually.
For describing the elliptic diagrams

\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
and
\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
, we need to determine
the following data:

•

the shape of these diagrams (nodes and edges);

•

the color of nodes in them.

Especially, to determine the shapes of them, we need to compute the exponents
of the nodes in

|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
and
|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)|
,
respectively. Furthermore, to compute the exponents, we have to determine
the non-reduced counting numbers for these nodes
(c.f. Definition
8.1
).
For
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
, every data is determined in
\lx@sectionsign

9.2

(see Proposition
9.3
), and for
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
,
they are done in
\lx@sectionsign

9.3
(see Proposition
9.8
).
Compiling these results, we have an answer for Problem 2 in

\lx@sectionsign

9.4
(Proposition
9.11
).

9.2.
Structure of
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}

First, we treat the reduced mERS
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
.
Let
(\Pi_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},\Pi_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})
be a paired simple system of

(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
and
\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
be its elliptic diagram.
Denote
\Pi_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}=\{\beta_{0},\ldots,\beta_{l}\}
.
By the definition, the linearly independent subset

\Pi_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}=\big\{(\beta_{0})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime},\ldots,(\beta_{l})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big\}
is given by

(\beta_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}=\big(\beta_{i}\big)^{pr}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\quad\text{for }0\leq i\leq l,

where
(\,\cdot\,)^{pr}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}:R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}\to R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
is the prime map for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
.

The subdiagram of
\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
whose nodes consist of all elements of

\Pi_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
is denoted by
\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
. Then, we
have

(127)

|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|=|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|\amalg|\Gamma_{m}^{\uparrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)^{\ast}|.

On the other hand, let
(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})
be a paired simple system of

(R,G)
and denote
\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}=\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{l}\}
. Note that

\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
is a simple system of
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
. Since
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
is reduced, one has

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}\cap\frac{1}{2}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}})_{l}=\emptyset
, and

\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
satisfies the condition (
56
) as a simple
system of
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
, automatically. Therefore, by
Proposition
7.23
,
we may assume

\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}=\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}\quad\text{and}\quad\beta_{i}=\alpha_{i}\quad\text{for }0\leq i\leq l.

That is, for a fixed simple system

\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}=\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{l}\}
, we may assume that
a paired simple system
(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}},\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}})
has the following forms:

\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}=\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{l}\}\quad\text{and}\quad\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}=\big\{(\alpha_{0})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime},\ldots,(\alpha_{l})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big\}.

Note that, since
(\alpha_{i})^{\prime}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}=(\alpha_{i})^{pr}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}

is the image of
\alpha_{i}\in\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
under the prime map

(\,\cdot\,)^{pr}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}:R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}\to R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
,

it
does not
coincide
with
\alpha_{i}^{\prime}=(\alpha_{i})^{pr}\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
in general,
where
(\,\cdot\,)^{pr}:R\to R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
is the prime map for
R
.
Here and after, when we regard
\alpha_{i}\in\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}\,(0\leq i\leq l)

as an element of
\Pi_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
, we denote it
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}
.

The goal of
\lx@sectionsign

9.2
is the following proposition.

Proposition 9.3
.

(1)
The explicit forms of
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\,(0\leq i\leq l)
are
given by

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}2\alpha_{i}-k(\alpha_{i})a&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\not\in R$ and
$\frac{1}{2}\overline{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}}\in R/G$},\\
\alpha_{i}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(2)
Let
k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}(\alpha)
be the counting number of

\alpha\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
as an element of the reduced mERS
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
. One has

\displaystyle k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)

\displaystyle=k(\alpha_{i})\quad\text{for every }0\leq i\leq l,

\displaystyle k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2k(\alpha_{i})&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\not\in R$ and
$\frac{1}{2}\overline{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}}\in R/G$},\\
k(\alpha_{i})&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(3)
The explicit forms of
\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\in\big(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)^{\ast}\,(0\leq i\leq l)
are given by

\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in R$},\\
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(4)
For
0\leq i\leq l
, let
m_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}}
be the exponent of

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}
. One has

m_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}}=m_{\alpha_{i}}\quad\text{for every }0\leq i\leq l.

As a by-product, it follows that the correspondence

\begin{array}[]{lllllllll}\alpha_{i}&\longmapsto&(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}&\text{for }\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R,G)|,\\[5.0pt]
(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}&\longmapsto&\big((\alpha_{j})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}&\text{for }(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\uparrow}(R,G)^{\ast}|\end{array}

gives a bijection from
|\Gamma(R,G)|
to
|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
.

(5)
The color of a node in
|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
is determined by the following
rules.

•

For a white node in
|\Gamma(R,G)|
, the corresponding node in

|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
is a white node also.

•

If
\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{\sharp}^{g}\,\,(\sharp=g\text{ or }b)
, both

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}
and
\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}
are gray nodes.

•

If
\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{\sharp}^{b}\,\,(\sharp=g\text{ or }b)
, both

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}
and
\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}
are white nodes.

•

If
\alpha_{i}\in|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|
,

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}
is a white node.

After some preparations, we will prove this proposition at the end of

\lx@sectionsign

9.2
.
Let us start with the next lemma.

Lemma 9.4
.

For
\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}|
, the following two conditions are equivalent.

(a)

\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in R
.

(b)

\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{g}^{b}\amalg|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{b}^{b}\amalg|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma^{\downarrow}_{m})^{\mathrm{nw}}|
.

Proof.

If
\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow}|
, the condition (a) is equivalent to

\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{g}^{b}\amalg|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{b}^{b}
.
Otherwise,
\alpha_{i}
belongs to

|\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow}|
.
By (
86
), condition (a)
implies
\overline{\alpha_{i}^{\prime}}=2\overline{\alpha_{i}}\in R/G
.
Therefore, we have

\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow}|\cap|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|=|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma^{\downarrow}_{m})^{\mathrm{nw}}|
. Conversely,
assume
\alpha_{i}\in|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma^{\downarrow}_{m})^{\mathrm{nw}}|
.
By Lemma
8.8
(2), Proposition
8.9
(1) (c) and
(3) (b), we have
\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in R
. Thus, we completed the proof.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

Assume
\frac{1}{2}\overline{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}}\in R/G
.
If
\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\not\in R
,
the node
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
is a gray node in
\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
.
Otherwise, it is a white node.

Proof.

First, assume
\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\not\in R
and

\frac{1}{2}\overline{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}}\in R/G
.
By (
70
), (
84
) and
(
88
), we have

(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}=2\alpha_{i}+k(\alpha_{i})a.

This formula says that
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}
belongs to
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
. As a
by-product, we get

2\overline{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}}=2\overline{\alpha_{i}}=\overline{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}}\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}/G.

Therefore, the node
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|

is a colored node in
|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
. Since
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
is reduced,
it should be a gray node.

Second, assume
\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in R
. Since

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}=\alpha_{i}\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
and
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
is reduced, we have

2(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}=2\alpha_{i}\not\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
.
Therefore,
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}=\alpha_{i}
is a white node
in
|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
, and the lemma is obtained.
∎

Lemma 9.6
.

(1)
One has
k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}(\alpha_{i})=k(\alpha_{i})
for every
0\leq i\leq l
.

(2)
For every
0\leq i\leq l
,

(128)

\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in R$},\\
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(3)
One has
m_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}}=m_{\alpha_{i}}
for every
0\leq i\leq l
.

Proof.

(1) Since
R(\alpha_{i})=R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})
for every
0\leq i\leq l
(see Proposition

9.1
), we have statement (1), immediately.

(2) By (
70
) and Lemma

7.14
(1), we have

(129)

\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}=\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}\big\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}+k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)a\big\}.

On the other hand, we get

\displaystyle(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}+k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}+k^{\mathrm{nr}}(\alpha_{i})a\quad(\because\ \eqref{nr-counting-number})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}:\alpha_{i})_{G}}\,(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\quad(\because\ \text{\eqref{defn:nr-countings_alpha'-ast} and Lemma
\ref{lemma:nr-countings1} (1)}).

Hence, by (
129
), we have

\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}=\dfrac{\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}}{((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}:\alpha_{i})_{G}}\,(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}.

By the definition of the two prime maps and Lemma

9.5
, we get

\displaystyle\dfrac{\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}}{((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}:\alpha_{i})_{G}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}&\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{if $(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|$ is a white node}\\
\text{\quad and $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R,G)|$ is not a white node},\end{array}\\
1&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{g}^{b},\,|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{b}^{b}$ or
$|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma^{\downarrow}_{m})^{\mathrm{nw}}|$
},\\
1&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

By Lemma
9.4
, we have the desired result.

(3) Note that
I_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}},(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)=I_{R}(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i})
. By the definition of the exponents, we have

\displaystyle m_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}}=

\displaystyle\dfrac{I_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}},(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)}{2k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)}n_{\alpha_{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{I_{R}(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i})}{2k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)}n_{\alpha_{i}}=\dfrac{k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)}{k^{\mathrm{nr}}(\alpha_{i})}m_{\alpha_{i}}.

Here,
k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)
is the non-reduced counting number
of
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}=\alpha_{i}
.
Therefore, for proving statement (2), it is enough to show that

(130)

k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)=k^{\mathrm{nr}}(\alpha_{i}).

Applying Corollary
7.16
to the mERSs
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
, we get

\displaystyle k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\not\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}$ and
$\frac{1}{2}\overline{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}}\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}/G$},\\
k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}k(\alpha_{i})&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\not\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}$ and
$\frac{1}{2}\overline{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}}\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}/G$},\\
k(\alpha_{i})&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that the second equality follows from statement (1).

Claim.

For
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
,
the following two conditions are equivalent
:

(a)

\frac{1}{2}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\not\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}

and

\frac{1}{2}\overline{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}}\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}/G
.

(b)

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}
is a gray node.

Proof.

First, since
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
is reduced, note that the condition

\frac{1}{2}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\not\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}

is satisfied for every
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
.
Second, by
the equivalence (
86
) for

(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\overline{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}}\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}/G\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad 2\overline{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}}\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}/G.

The right hand side means that the node

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
is not
a white node. Since
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
is reduced, it should be a gray node.
∎

Lemma
9.5
asserts that the following
two conditions
are equivalent:

(i)

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)|
is a gray node;

(ii)

(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in|(\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}})^{*}|
is a gray node.

Since the later means that
\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\not\in R
and

\frac{1}{2}\overline{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}}\in R/G
, we get

\displaystyle k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\mathrm{nr}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}k(\alpha_{i})&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\not\in R$ and
$\frac{1}{2}\overline{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}}\in R/G$},\\
k(\alpha_{i})&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

By Corollary
7.16
for the mERS
(R,G)
, one has that
the right hand side
of the quality above coincides with
k^{\mathrm{nr}}(\alpha_{i})
.
Thus, (
130
) is obtained, and the proof of statement
(3) is completed.
∎

Now, let us prove Proposition
9.3
.

Proof of Proposition
9.3
.
The first half of statement (2), statements (3) and (4) are already proved
in Lemma
9.6
(1), (2) and (3), respectively. Statement
(5) is an immediate consequence of Lemma
9.5
.
Applying Proposition
7.17
to the reduced mERS
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
,
the second half of statement (2) is obtained.
Thus, the remaining is to prove statement (1).

By the definition of the

\ast
-operation, we have

(131)

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}=\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}-k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)a.

The explicit form of the first term of the right hand side is already known in
statement (3).
The explicit form of the second term is also known in
statement (2).
Substituting them to (
131
), statement (1) is obtained
by Corollary
7.16
and Proposition

7.17
.
Thus, we completed the proof of the proposition.
\square

9.3.
Structure of
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}

Next, we study the reduced mERS
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
. Define

\begin{array}[]{lll}|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}&\!\!\!\!=|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{g}\amalg|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{b}\amalg|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|&\text{(the set of all black nodes in $|\Gamma^{\downarrow}|$)},\\[3.0pt]
|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}&\!\!\!\!=|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{g}\amalg|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}_{m}|_{g}^{b}&\text{(the set of all gray nodes in $|\Gamma^{\downarrow}|$)}.\end{array}

Here, we recall that
\Gamma^{\downarrow}=\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R,G)
.
Then, the following decomposition of
|\Gamma^{\downarrow}|
is obtained:

(132)

|\Gamma^{\downarrow}|=|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|\amalg|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}\amalg|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}.

Set

\begin{array}[]{lllll}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(i)&\!\!\!\!=R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|$},\\[3.0pt]
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(j)&\!\!\!\!=R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j})\cup R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})&\text{if $\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|=|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}\amalg|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}$},\end{array}

where
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{i})
,
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(\alpha_{j})
and

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}((\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast})
are subsets of
R
introduced in Proposition

9.1
.
By (
126
), we have

(133)

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}=\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(i)\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(j)\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}}R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(j)\Big).

Take the image of both sides under the canonical projection
\pi_{G}:F\to F/G
.
By using the explicit descriptions of
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(i)
and
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(j)
in Proposition

9.1
, we have

(134)

\displaystyle R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|}\overline{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(i)}\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}}\overline{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(j)}\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}}\overline{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}(j)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|}W(R/G)(\overline{\alpha_{i}})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}}W(R/G)(2\overline{\alpha_{j}})\Big)

\displaystyle\bigcup\Big(\bigcup_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}}\Big(W(R/G)(\overline{\alpha_{j}})\bigcup W(R/G)(2\overline{\alpha_{j}})\Big)\Big)\quad(\because\ \text{Lemma \ref{lemma:paired-reduced-basis} (1)}).

Recall the reduced pair
((R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G)^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G)^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})
of the affine root system
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G
.
The formula (
134
) implies that

•

the set
\{\overline{\alpha_{i}}\}_{\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|}\amalg\{2\overline{\alpha_{j}}\}_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}}\amalg\{\overline{\alpha_{j}}\}_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}}
gives a simple system of

(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G)^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
;

•

the set
\{\overline{\alpha_{i}}\}_{\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|}\amalg\{2\overline{\alpha_{j}}\}_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}}\amalg\{2\overline{\alpha_{j}}\}_{\alpha_{j}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}}
gives a simple system of

(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G)^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
.

Motivated by these facts, we define a linearly independent subset

\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\}_{0\leq i\leq l}
of
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
by

(135)

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}=\begin{cases}2\alpha_{i}&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}$},\\
\alpha_{i}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

The above facts assert that the image

\{\overline{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}\}_{0\leq i\leq l}\subset R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G

is a simple system of
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G
. Similar to the case of

, one has

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}\cap\frac{1}{2}(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})_{l}=\emptyset
, because
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
is reduced.
Therefore, the set
\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\}_{0\leq i\leq l}
is a simple system of

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
which satisfies condition (
56
). Define

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}=\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)^{pr}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\quad\text{for }0\leq i\leq l,

where
(\,\cdot\,)^{pr}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}\to R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}

is the prime map for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
.

Then, the pair

\big(\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\}_{0\leq i\leq l},\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\}_{0\leq i\leq l}\big)

is a paired simple system of
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
.
By Proposition
7.23
, any paired simple
system
(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}},\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}})
of
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)

is the image of the pair
\big(\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\}_{0\leq i\leq l},\\
\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\}_{0\leq i\leq l}\big)
under an automorphism of
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
.
Thus, we may assume that

(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}},\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}})=\big(\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\}_{0\leq i\leq l},\{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\}_{0\leq i\leq l}\big).

Let
a^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}
be a generator of the lattice
Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G
of rank
1
.
We may assume that
a^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}a
.
More precisely, we have

(136)

a^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}=[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]a.

In the following, we determine the elliptic diagram
\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
of

(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
. Let us start with the following lemma.

Lemma 9.7
.

(1)
Let
k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}(\alpha)
be the counting number of

\alpha\in R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
as an element of the reduced mERS
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)

(137)

[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]\,k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)=\begin{cases}2k(\alpha_{i})&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in R$},\\
k(\alpha_{i})&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(138)

[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]\,k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)=k(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})\quad\text{for every}\quad 0\leq i\leq l,

(2)
The explicit forms of
\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\in\big(\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)^{\ast}\,(0\leq i\leq l)
are given by

\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}=(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}.

(3)
The explicit forms of

are given by

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}=\alpha_{i}^{\prime}.

Proof.

(1) The formula (

) is an easy consequence of the description
(

) of
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}

).
Note that, since

(139)

\begin{split}&k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)\\

2k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)&\text{if $(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}$ is a gray node in $|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)|$}.\end{cases}\end{split}

By the definition of
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}
, we have

(140)

\text{$(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}$ is a gray node in $\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)$}&\Longleftrightarrow&\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}.\end{array}\right.

Therefore, by (
139
) and
(
140
), we have

\displaystyle[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]\,k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]\,k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)\times\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|\amalg|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}$},\\
2&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}$}.\end{cases}

Furthermore, (

4k(\alpha_{i})&\text{if }\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{b}.\end{cases}

By Proposition
7.17
for
(R,G)
, this coincides with

k((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast})=k(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})
. Thus, we have (
138
).

(2) Note that the formula

(141)

k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{nr}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)=k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)

2k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{nr}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)&\text{if $(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}$ is a gray node in $\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)$}.\end{cases}

This formula and (

(

).

Hence, by (
70
) and (
141
),
we get

(142)

\displaystyle\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{*}=\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{*}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{mc}}\big)_{G}(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}+k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)a^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}.

)
of

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}&\text{if $(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}$ is a white node},\\
2(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}&\text{if $(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}$ is a gray node},\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\alpha_{i}&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|$},\\
2\alpha_{i}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

Furthermore, by (

) and (

), we get

\displaystyle k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)a^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2k(\alpha_{i})a&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{b}\amalg|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{b}\amalg|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|$},\\

This coincides with
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

(3)
By the definition of the
\ast
-operation and (

), we have

\alpha_{i}^{\prime}=(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}-k((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast})a\quad{and}\quad(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}=\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}-k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)a^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}.

Compiling these four formulas, we have the desired result.
∎

For
0\leq i\leq l
, let
m_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}
be the exponent
of
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\in\Pi_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
, as an element of

(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
. Its explicit form is given by

(143)

m_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}=\dfrac{I_{R^{{}^{m}c}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}},(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)}{2k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{nr}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)}n_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}},

where
\big\{n_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big\}_{0\leq i\leq l}
is the family
of positive integers such that

\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:=\sum_{0\leq i\leq l}n_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\in Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap\mathrm{rad}(I)

is primitive. To determine the shape of the elliptic diagram
\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
,
we need to compute the exponents
m_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\ (0\leq l\leq l)

explicitly.

Our goal is the following proposition.

Proposition 9.8
.

(1)
One has

m^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}=\dfrac{[Q(R)\cap\mathrm{rad}(I):Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap\mathrm{rad}(I)]}{(I_{R}:I_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}})}\,m_{\alpha_{i}}\quad\text{for every }0\leq i\leq l.

As a by-product, it follows that the correspondence

\begin{array}[]{lllllllll}\alpha_{i}&\longmapsto&(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}&\text{for }\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\downarrow}(R,G)|,\\[5.0pt]
(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}&\longmapsto&\big((\alpha_{j})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}&\text{for }(\alpha_{j}^{\prime})^{\ast}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\uparrow}(R,G)^{\ast}|\end{array}

gives a bijection from
|\Gamma(R,G)|
to
|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)|
.

(2)
The color of a node in
|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)|
is determined by the following
rules.

•

For a white node in
|\Gamma(R,G)|
, the corresponding node in

|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)|
is a white node also.

•

If
\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{\sharp}\,\,(\sharp=g\text{ or }b)
, both

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}
and
\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}
are gray nodes.

•

If
\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{\sharp}\,\,(\sharp=g\text{ or }b)
, both

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}
and
\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}
are white nodes.

•

If
\alpha_{i}\in|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|
,

(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}
is a white node.

Before proving the proposition, we need some preparations. Take the image

\overline{\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}=\pi_{G}(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}})

of
\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}
under the canonical projection
\pi_{G}:F\longrightarrow F/G
:

\overline{\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}=\sum_{0\leq i\leq l}n_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\overline{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}\in Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}/G)\cap\mathrm{rad}(I_{F/G}).

Recall that the element
\delta_{b}=\sum_{0\leq i\leq l}n_{\alpha_{i}}\alpha_{i}\in Q(W([)\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}].\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}})\cap\mathrm{rad}(I)
introduced in
(
68
), and its image

of
\delta_{b}
under the
canonical projection
\pi_{G}:
8
8

8

In
\lx@sectionsign

,

is denoted by
\delta
.

\overline{\delta_{b}}=\sum_{0\leq i\leq l}n_{\alpha_{i}}\overline{\alpha_{i}}\in Q(R/G)\cap\mathrm{rad}(I_{F/G}).

Since

\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\equiv\big(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\delta_{b}\big)_{G}\,\delta_{b}\ (\mathrm{mod}\,G).

Lemma 9.9
.

For every
0\leq i\leq l
, we have

(145)

n_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}=\dfrac{\big(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\delta_{b}\big)_{G}}{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}}\,n_{\alpha_{i}}.

Proof.

Since
\alpha_{i}^{\prime}\equiv(\alpha_{i}^{\prime}:\alpha_{i})_{G}\alpha_{i}\,(\mathrm{mod}\,G)

for every
0\leq i\leq l
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{b}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{0\leq i\leq l}\dfrac{n_{\alpha_{i}}}{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime}:\alpha_{i})_{G}}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}\ (\mathrm{mod}\,G).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{0\leq i\leq l}\dfrac{n_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}}(\alpha_{i})^{\prime}_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\ (\mathrm{mod}\,G)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq i\leq l}\dfrac{n_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}{\big(\alpha_{i}^{\prime}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}\quad(\because\,\text{Lemma \ref{lemma:Gamma(R'',G)} (3)}).

These two equalities and (
144
) imply

n_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}=\big(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\delta_{b}\big)_{G}\dfrac{\big(\alpha_{i}^{\prime}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}}{(\alpha_{i}^{\prime}:\alpha_{i})_{G}}\,n_{\alpha_{i}}=\dfrac{\big(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\delta_{b}\big)_{G}}{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}}\,n_{\alpha_{i}},

as desired.
∎

Lemma 9.10
.

For every
0\leq i\leq l
, we have

(146)

\dfrac{k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{nr}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)}{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}}=\dfrac{k^{nr}(\alpha_{i})}{[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]}.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.14
(1) for
the mERS
(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
and (
141
), we have

\displaystyle k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{nr}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{nr}\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}\big)}{\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}}=\dfrac{k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)}{\big(\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{\prime}\big)^{\ast}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)}{\big((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}}\quad(\because\ \text{Lemma \ref{lemma:Gamma(R'',G)} (2)}).

Note that

\displaystyle\big((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)_{G}=

\displaystyle\big((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}\big(\alpha_{i}:(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)_{G},

\displaystyle\big((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1&\text{if }\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|,\\
2&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Therefore, by (
137
), we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{nr}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)}{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}}=\dfrac{k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)}{\big((\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast},\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{k(\alpha_{i})}{[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]}\times\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in$}\begin{array}[]{l}|\Gamma^{\mathrm{w}}|\amalg|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{b}\amalg|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{b}\\
\amalg|(\Gamma^{\downarrow}\setminus\Gamma_{m}^{\downarrow})^{\mathrm{nw}}|,\end{array}\\[3.0pt]
1/2&\text{if $\alpha_{i}\in|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{g}^{g}\amalg|\Gamma_{m}^{\mathrm{nw}}|_{b}^{g},$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{k^{nr}(\alpha_{i})}{[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]}\quad(\because\ \text{Proposition \ref{prop:nr-countings1}}),

as desired.
∎

Now, let us prove Proposition
9.8
.

Proof of Proposition
9.8
.
(1) One has

\displaystyle I_{R^{{}^{m}c}}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}},(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)

\displaystyle=\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}^{2}I_{R^{{}^{m}c}}(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i})

\displaystyle=\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}^{2}(I_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:I_{R})I_{R}(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i}).

Substitute this formula and (
145
)
to the right hand side of (
143
), we have

\displaystyle m_{(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\alpha_{i}\big)^{2}\cdot I_{R}(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i})}{(I_{R}:I_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}})}\times\dfrac{1}{2k_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}^{nr}\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}\big)}\times\dfrac{\big(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\delta_{b}\big)_{G}}{\big((\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\alpha_{i}\big)_{G}}\,n_{\alpha_{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]\cdot\big(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\delta_{b}\big)_{G}}{(I_{R}:I_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}})}\cdot\dfrac{I_{R}(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i})}{2k^{nr}(\alpha_{i})}\,n_{\alpha_{i}}\quad(\because\ \eqref{eq:nr-counging-Rmc})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]\cdot\big(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\delta_{b}\big)_{G}}{(I_{R}:I_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}})}\,m_{\alpha_{i}}.

Since

[Q(R)\cap G:Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap G]\cdot\big(\delta_{b}^{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}:\delta_{b}\big)_{G}=[Q(R)\cap\mathrm{rad}(I):Q(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}})\cap\mathrm{rad}(I)],

we have statement (1).

(2) We already know that

(i)

the color of
(\alpha_{i})_{R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}}
in

|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)|
is determined according to the rule in statement (2);

(ii)

the diagram
\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
has the same shape as
\Gamma(R,G)

(by (1));

(iii)

there is no black node in
|\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)|
.

Under these conditions, the diagram
\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
is uniquely determined,
with the rule in statement (2).

\square

9.4.
Diagrammatic interpretation

The results of Propositions
9.3
and
9.8
are translated in diagrammatic language as
follows.

Proposition 9.11
.

(1)
Both of the elliptic diagrams
\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}},G)
and

\Gamma(R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}},G)
have the same shape as
\Gamma(R,G)
.

(2)
The colors of nodes of them are given as follows
:

transform the black nodes and the gray nodes by the following
4
rules.

(D0)

If a black node

\alpha_{i}

is only connected to white nodes,
replace it

i)

with a white node

\alpha_{i}

for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
and

ii)

with a white node

2\alpha_{i}

for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
.

(D1)

Replace the unit

\alpha_{i}
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

i)

with

\alpha_{i}
\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
and

ii)

with

2\alpha_{i}
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
.

(D2)

Replace the unit

\alpha_{i}
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

i)

with

\alpha_{i}
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
and

ii)

with

2\alpha_{i}
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
.

(D3)

Replace the unit

\alpha_{i}
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

i)

with

\alpha_{i}
\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
and

ii)

with

\alpha_{i}
(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})^{\ast}

for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
.

Note that the newly obtained white nodes represent short roots for

R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
and long roots for
R^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}
.

(R,G)
with non-reduced quotient
R/G
, belonging to

(F,I_{F})
where

is a symmetric bilinear form with signature
(\overset{+}{l},\overset{0}{2},\overset{-}{0})
.

(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\cdots,\varepsilon_{l},b,a)
of

as in (

).

,

roots in
\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
,

ii)

roots in
\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{m}}^{c}}\setminus\Pi^{\,\underline{\mathrm{d}}^{c}}
.

for

,

.

10.5.13.
Type
C^{\vee}C_{l}^{(4)\ast_{0}}

(l\geq 1)

(1)

(C^{\vee}C_{l}^{(4)\ast_{0}})^{\vee}=C^{\vee}C_{l}^{(1)\ast_{0}}.

(2)

,

,

.

,

l=1
l\geq 2

Appendix

In this appendix, for the sake of reader’s convenience, we collect some root data of finite and affine root systems.
In addition, we also provide some root data of a marked elliptic root system
(R,G)

whose affine quotient is reduced.
For an affine and elliptic root system
R
, we also describe the decomposition of
R
into
W(R)
-orbits. Such information might be useful in applications.

A.
Finite root systems

In this section, we provide necessary informations on finite root systems, including the unique non-reduced one.

Each root system of rank
l
is a subset of the vector space
\mathbb{R}^{l}
with the scalar product
I
, and let
{\varepsilon_{i},1\leq i\leq l}
be an orthonormal basis with respect to
I
.

A.1.
Reduced types

A.1.1.
A_{l}

(l\geq 1)

(1)

Roots:

R(A_{l})=\{\pm(\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{j})\,|1\leq i<j\leq l\}.

(2)

Simple roots:
\alpha_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{i+1}\;(1\leq i\leq l)
.

(3)

Highest root:
\theta=\varepsilon_{1}-\varepsilon_{l+1}=\sum_{i=1}^{l}\alpha_{i}
.

(4)

Dynkin diagram:

\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{l-1}
\alpha_{l}

A.1.2.
B_{l}

(l\geq 2)

(1)

Roots:

R(B_{l})=\{\pm\varepsilon_{i}\pm\varepsilon_{j}\,|1\leq i<j\leq l\}\cup\{\pm\varepsilon_{i}\,|\,1\leq i\leq l\},

(2)

Simple roots:
\alpha_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{i+1}\;(1\leq i<l)
and
\alpha_{l}=\varepsilon_{l}
.

(3)

Highest root:
\theta=\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{2}=\alpha_{1}+2(\alpha_{2}+\cdots+\alpha_{l})
.

(4)

Dynkin diagram:

\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{l-1}
\alpha_{l}
2

A.1.3.
C_{l}

(l\geq 2)

(1)

Roots:

R(C_{l})=\{\pm\varepsilon_{i}\pm\varepsilon_{j}\,|1\leq i<j\leq l\}\cup\{\pm 2\varepsilon_{i}\,|\,1\leq i\leq l\},

(2)

Simple roots:
\alpha_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{i+1}\;(1\leq i<l)
and
\alpha_{l}=2\varepsilon_{l}
.

(3)

Highest root:
\theta=2\varepsilon_{1}=2(\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{l-1})+\alpha_{l}
.

(4)

Dynkin diagram:

\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{l-1}
\alpha_{l}
2

A.1.4.
D_{l}

(l\geq 3)

(1)

Roots:

R(D_{l})=\{\pm\varepsilon_{i}\pm\varepsilon_{j}\,|1\leq i<j\leq l\},

(2)

Simple roots:
\alpha_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{i+1}\;(1\leq i<l)
and
\alpha_{l}=\varepsilon_{l-1}+\varepsilon_{l}
.

(3)

Highest root:
\theta=\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{2}=\alpha_{1}+2(\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{l-2})+\alpha_{l-1}+\alpha_{l}
.

(4)

Dynkin diagram:

\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{l-2}
\alpha_{l-1}
\alpha_{l}

\displaystyle R(E_{6})=

\displaystyle\{\pm\varepsilon_{i}\pm\varepsilon_{j}\,|1\leq i<j\leq 5\}

\displaystyle\cup\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\varepsilon_{8}-\varepsilon_{7}-\varepsilon_{6}+\sum_{i=1}^{5}(-1)^{\nu(i)}\varepsilon_{i}\right)\,\left|\,\sum_{i=1}^{5}\nu(i):\,\text{even}\,\right\},\right.

(2)

Simple roots:
\alpha_{1}=\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{8})-\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{7}),\;\alpha_{2}=\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{2},

simple r roots

B.
Affine root systems

In this section, we provide necessary informations on the affine root systems.
In particular, this includes the four non-reduced cases classified by I. Macdonald
[
Mac72
]
. For the reduced affine root systems, we follow the nomenclatures due to Moody
[
Moo69
]
, except for the case
BC_{1}^{(2)}
which was called
A_{1}^{(2)}
there.

Each affine root system of rank
{l+1}
is a subset of the vector space
\mathbb{R}^{l+1}
with the symmetric bilinear form
I
with signature
(l,1,0)
. Let
\varepsilon_{i}\,(1\leq i\leq l)\,\text{and}\,b
be a basis of
\mathbb{R}^{l+1}
with the pairing given by

I(\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j})=\delta_{i,j}\,,I(\varepsilon_{i},b)=0\,,I(b,b)=0.

B.1.
Reduced types

B.1.1.
A_{l}^{(1)}

(l\geq 1)

(1)

Real roots:
R(A_{l}^{(1)})=R(A_{l})+\mathbb{Z}b
.

(2)

Simple roots:
\alpha_{0}=b-\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{l+1},\;\alpha_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{i+1}\;(1\leq i\leq l)
.

(3)

b=\sum_{i=0}^{l}\alpha_{i}
.

(4)

Dynkin diagram:

i)

l=1
:
\alpha_{0}
\infty
\alpha_{1}

ii)

l\geq 2
:
\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{l-1}
\alpha_{l}
\alpha_{0}

(5)

W
-orbits:

i)

l=1
:
R(A_{1}^{(1)})=W(\alpha_{0})\amalg W(\alpha_{1})
.

ii)

l\geq 2
: single
W
-orbit.

(l\geq 3)

.

(2)

\alpha_{0}=b-\varepsilon_{1}-\varepsilon_{2},\;\alpha_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{i+1}\;(1\leq i<l)

Simple roots:

\alpha_{l}=\varepsilon_{l}
.

(3)

b=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1}+2(\alpha_{2}+\cdots+\alpha_{l})
.

(4)

(5)

-orbits:

C.
MERSs with reduced affine quotient

We obtained the classification of mERSs
(R,G)
, belonging to
(F,I)
, with reduced
R/G
. As a consequence, one sees that the classification due to K. Saito
[
Sai85
]
is complete in this case. For the sake of completeness, let us collect several important root data for each marked elliptic root system
(R,G)
:

(1)

The tier numbers
t_{1}(R,G)
and
t_{2}(R,G)
and the marked dual root system
(R^{\vee},G)
,

(2)

Root system
R
,

(3)

Simple roots of
(R,G)
,

(4)

Counting and Exponents,

(5)

Elliptic diagram,

(6)

W
-orbits.

Here, we fix a basis
(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\cdots,\varepsilon_{l},b,a)
of
F
as in (
16
).

C.1.1.
Type
A_{l}^{(1,1)}\;(l\geq 1)

(1)

t_{1}(A_{l}^{(1,1)})=1
,
t_{2}(A_{l}^{(1,1)})=1
and

(A_{l}^{(1,1)})^{\vee}=A_{l}^{(1,1)}
.

(2)

R(A_{l}^{(1,1)})=R(A_{l})+\mathbb{Z}a+\mathbb{Z}b
.

(3)

\alpha_{0}=-\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{l+1}+b,\;\alpha_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}-\varepsilon_{i+1}\,(1\leq i\leq l)
.

(4)

k(\alpha_{i})=1
(
0\leq i\leq l
).

m_{i}=1
(

).
